# Claims and evidence ledger

This is the canonical list of what we know, what we think we know, and what we
do not know. Other researchers and agents should use these IDs when referring
to a finding, disputing it, or extending it.

## Status labels

| Label | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **VERIFIED** | Reproduced directly from the sample bytes with a script; another person can re-run the check. |
| **OBSERVED** | Seen in the data, but the interpretation is not proven. |
| **HYPOTHESIS** | A plausible explanation that fits current evidence but has not been confirmed. |
| **UNKNOWN** | No reliable answer yet. |

Confidence: **H** = high, **M** = medium, **L** = low.

When a claim changes status, update this file and note the evidence in the
commit message. Do not silently promote a hypothesis to a fact.

Evidence should cite the relevant `docs/experiments/EXP-...` record and/or a
`tools/` command. The experiment log is append-only; if a result is corrected,
add a new experiment and mark `Supersedes` / `Superseded by`.

## Card / image

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-001 | The EU 2011 image is 8,057,257,984 bytes with SHA-256 `105e2032…76307`. | VERIFIED | H | `sha256sum`, `stat` |
| C-002 | It has an MBR with exactly one partition, type `0x0b`, start LBA 204802. | VERIFIED | H | `fdisk -l`, `7z l`, MBR bytes at `0x1be` |
| C-003 | The FAT32 volume label is `QSD-309-101`; cluster size 65,536 bytes. | VERIFIED | H | FAT boot sector, `7z l` |
| C-004 | The filesystem contains 39 directories, 480 files, 5,173,965,667 bytes. | VERIFIED | H | `7z x`, `find`, `du` |
| C-005 | `VER_INFO/HDD_INFO.TXT` contains six CRLF-separated lines: `QSD-302-100`, `QSD-309-101`, `02-110100-00`, `SDC08-EVEUR`, `11-05`, `470323501005`. | VERIFIED | H | hexdump of the 72-byte file |
| C-006 | The 100 MB before the FAT partition contains Clarion records (`CLARION ID` at `0x200`) and a firmware container at `0x200000`. | VERIFIED | H | raw bytes, see [firmware.md](../format/firmware.md) |
| C-007 | The 402,652,160-byte post-partition tail is almost entirely non-zero (400,365,370 bytes). | VERIFIED | H | byte scan |
| C-008 | The purpose of the post-partition tail is unknown. | UNKNOWN | — | no successful parser yet |
| C-009 | The map validity/activation mechanism (checksum, signature, CID binding, version code) is unknown. | UNKNOWN | — | no matching code or test yet |

## Common file header

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-010 | Most map files start with a 512-byte header containing the UTF-16**BE** strings `CLARION`, `HITACHI`, `CLA-NAVI05-01`, `FORMAT VERSION X08-02-00`, `DATA VERSION 11/06/27/01`, `V05.00.00`. | VERIFIED | H | hexdump of `MMAIN001.DAT`, `MAPAL001/*.DAT`, `RDSTM001.DAT`, `BICON001.DAT`, decompressed `REFER001` payloads |
| C-011 | The header contains a file-type-specific binary descriptor at `0x180`. | VERIFIED | H | identical prefix `ff ff 24 ff 12 c0 05 00 fb 50 0f f0`, then per-type bytes |
| C-012 | The meaning of the descriptor fields is unknown. | UNKNOWN | — | not decoded |
| C-013 | `DRSTR001.DAT` does not use this 512-byte header. | VERIFIED | H | first bytes are a 32-bit count + binary records |

## `.cmp` / `.ind`

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-020 | `.ind` is little-endian: `u32 uncompressed_size, u32 compressed_size, u32 chunk_count, u32 chunk_size, u32 chunk_offset[chunk_count]`. | VERIFIED | H | matches all 194 pairs; see [cmp-ind.md](../format/cmp-ind.md) |
| C-021 | `ind_size == 16 + 4 * chunk_count`. | VERIFIED | H | 194/194 |
| C-022 | `chunk_size` is `0x10000` (65,536) in every examined file. | VERIFIED | H | 194/194 |
| C-023 | Each chunk is an independent zlib stream; concatenating decompressed chunks yields exactly `uncompressed_size` bytes. | VERIFIED | H | 194/194, `tools/cmpind.py verify` |
| C-024 | Naive `zlib.decompress()` on the whole `.cmp` only returns the first stream and silently truncates large files. | VERIFIED | H | `ADDRE001`: 65,536 of 79,360 bytes |
| C-025 | The decompressed payload still starts with the 512-byte header. | VERIFIED | H | `COORD001` decompressed size 4,608 = 512 + 4,096 |
| C-026 | The record format inside address/POI payloads is not decoded. | UNKNOWN | — | only strings such as `STREET`, `area code`, `street name`, `dndc-id` observed |

## `DRSTR001.DAT`

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-030 | `DRSTR001.DAT` has **no header**. The node table starts at `0x0000` with the root node `/`; the 2011 sample has 40 nodes. `0x2f` is the ASCII `/` of the root name, not a count. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-05-01; `40*40 + 324*32 = 11968` |
| C-031 | Node records are 40 bytes, name-first: `name[8]` at `+0x00`, `next_index` at `+0x14`, `parent_index+1` at `+0x16`, `entry_count` at `+0x18`, `listing_offset` at `+0x24`. | VERIFIED | H | 40/40 parse; invariants pass |
| C-032 | Listings are 32-byte entries: `name[13]` at `+0x00`, `type` at `+0x16` (0 = file, 2 = dir), `value` at `+0x1c`. | VERIFIED | H | 324 entries; names match FAT |
| C-033 | An earlier "one-node shift" was a parsing artefact: the table starts at `0x0000`, not `0x28`, and each node describes its own listing. | VERIFIED | H | corrected `tools/drstr.py`; listings tile exactly to EOF |
| C-034 | `next_index` is always `index + 1`; its purpose is unknown. | OBSERVED | H | all 40 nodes |
| C-035 | `CMP.LST` contains exactly `\SystemSD\REFER001\` (19 bytes, no newline). | VERIFIED | H | hexdump |
| C-036 | Entry `value`: for `.cmp` files it is the logical uncompressed size (`.ind`, 194/194); for `MAPAL001` tiles it is the **on-disk** size; for directories it is the recursive subtree total (verified for all 39 dirs). | VERIFIED | H | size invariants + FAT cross-check |
| C-037 | `MMAIN001.DAT` is **not** a manifest: no file names, offsets or checksums. It is a format/version descriptor (common header, per-language code list at `0x18c`, 3-section descriptor at `0x1ac`). | VERIFIED (negative) | H | full-byte analysis; CRC/Adler/sum probes negative |
| C-038 | There is no checksum/signature file on the card and no checksum field in `DRSTR001.DAT` or `MMAIN001.DAT`. Integrity of `REFER001` is zlib Adler-32 per 64 KiB chunk. | VERIFIED | H | 194/194 decompressions; field accounting |
| C-039 | The fixed data before the payload may extend to `0x5d6`, not 512 bytes: the 10-byte sequence `80 00 00 00 05 00 fb 50 0b 0b` appears at `0x5cc` in many files. | OBSERVED | M | cross-file hexdump; parser boundary not proven |

## `COORD001` / `ZCODE*` / `ICODE001`

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-110 | `COORD001` is a 33-entry table of region bounding boxes at `0x1010`, 14-byte big-endian records, sentinel-terminated; not a point list. | VERIFIED | H | `coord.py` fit |
| C-111 | Coordinate encoding: `lat = (x-32768)/192`, `lon = (y-52742)/128`; rms ~0.031° lat / ~0.073° lon over 33 regions. | VERIFIED | H | least-squares + outlier checks |
| C-112 | `ZCODE0nn` is a character/digit trie; postal-code text is never stored, so `strings` cannot extract it. | VERIFIED (negative) | H | full walk of ZCODE001/010/018 |
| C-113 | ZCODE directory entries are 12 bytes `(size, 0, tag, 0, count, offset)`; leaf records are 8 bytes `(x, y, sub_x, sub_y)` with 11-bit sub-positions. | VERIFIED | H | decoded records |
| C-114 | `ICODE001` starts with 33 per-country administrative schemas, then a flat ASCII place-name database. | VERIFIED | H | schema parse; 111 MB payload |
| C-115 | Region numbering 001–033 is alphabetical by German country name (001 Andorra … 033 United Kingdom). | OBSERVED | H | ICODE schemas + several bounding boxes |
| C-116 | The 2011 map covers Western/Central Europe only; Iceland, Malta, Cyprus, Turkey, Belarus, Ukraine, Russia, Moldova and the Balkans are absent. | OBSERVED | M | 33-region list |

Its twelve fields, in order, are location code, road number, road name, primary location, secondary location, area code, liner code, front code, rear code, urban attribute, exit/junction, inter-section code — the same twelve columns and order as the `LCTBL001` catalog. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M3.7-router-rdstm |
| C-883 | Decoded field offsets and accessors (`Navi.exe`): `+0x04` location code `0x003bbfe0`; `+0x06` area `0x003bc5e0`; `+0x08` liner `0x003bc628`; `+0x0a` front `0x003bc670`; `+0x0c` rear `0x003bc6b8`; `+0x0e` urban `0x003bc700`; `+0x10` inter `0x003bc8b8` (all u16); string fields with counts at `+0x12/+0x14/+0x16/+0x18/+0x1a` and blocks at `+0x1c/+0x40/+0xc4/+0x148/+0x1cc`: road number `0x003bc100`, road name `0x003bc26c`, primary `0x003bc3d8`, secondary `0x003bc548`, exit/junction `0x003bc820`. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M3.7-router-rdstm |
| C-884 | The raw record has no constant stride: fields are resolved through a per-record directory by `0x003f61bc(record,?,index)` over the schema lookup `0x003f51e0`, the length walk `0x003f56bc`/`0x003f5d98` (table at `record + 2*u16[record+2]`) and the offset accumulator `0x003f5e10`. Numeric fields use indexes 0, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11; the five string fields have dedicated extractors. This is the mechanism behind the framing drift at `0x10a0` (C-106) and replaces a fixed record size. | OBSERVED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M3.7-router-rdstm |

| C-886 | `RDSTM001` contains no speed, one-way, turn-restriction, toll or ferry attribute: the complete accessor surface for the record is one decoder (`0x003bc960`), twelve getters (C-883), `0x003bbf6c` and `sdl_TMC_SetRecordAddr`; none exposes such a field. | C-888 | `Navi.exe` uses the data-type code `0x14e4` in three code sites only: the descriptor lookup `0x003fa382` (`0x003f7384(type,out)`), the SDL request `0x003bcb48` and the volume/name selector `0x004125a8`; every other `0x14e4` byte in the module is data or a literal pool (e.g. `0x0009abe4`, `0x003fa5a8`). | VERIFIED | M | EXP-2026-11-06-M3.7-router-rdstm |
| C-889 | Consequence for M4.1–M4.3: `RDSTM001` must carry the twelve TMC fields for each record; speed/one-way/one-way restrictions cannot be sourced from it, so `M3.7`'s one-way/restriction part must come from the tile sections or `INTER001`/`FRWAY001`. | HYPOTHESIS | M | EXP-2026-11-06-M3.7-router-rdstm; C-886, C-887 |

## Tooling and offline contracts (2026-11-05)

Built without vendor data; every row states what is synthetic-only.

| C-542 | IMF v1 is a UTF-8 JSON Lines file (`imf=1`) with one header (pinned source + ODbL attribution), then nodes, ways and a restriction/boundary/route relation subset. Each record carries a stable `n\|w\|r<osm id>` id and a whole `BxxRyyR` cell; a way's `tile` is its first node's cell and `tiles` is the sorted set of all its nodes' cells. Speeds are integer cm/s, coordinates WGS84 degrees at 1e-7. Design only: the writer mapping is still undecided. | OBSERVED (design) | M | EXP-2026-11-05-44; `docs/research/intermediate-format.md`; Was proposed as C-542 in EXP-2026-11-05-44 |

Such a table must be stored in the priority order. | VERIFIED (addresses) | H | `sh4xref.py --to 0x1e3319c`; EXP-2026-11-06-M3.9-name-collation |
| C-1793 | `OBJDLL.dll!OBJ_Sort_cmpStr` (VA `0x0349d3b4`, export rva `0x1d3b4`) is a custom-table collation, not byte order and not locale: it keys each byte with `OBJ_Sort_getPriority` (VA `0x0349d368`) from the `u16` weight table at `0x03485400` (bytes `0x20`–`0x9f`, index `c-0x20`) and `0x03485500` (bytes `0xc0`–`0xff`, index `c-0xc0`), compares key sequences and breaks ties by length. The object is `0x620` bytes, read by `OBJ_PntInfo_readPntString` (`0x034a6350`, size word at `0x034a638e`) and written by `OBJ_PntInfo_writePntString` (`0x034a6494`). | VERIFIED | H | capstone; EXP-2026-11-06-M3.9-name-collation |

The LCTBL name streams are **not** sorted in the firmware weight order (240,619/477,173 adjacent pairs ascending = 50.4 %, 1/513 streams fully sorted), so a writer must not sort them. Supersedes the ISO-8859-2/"all Polish letters" part of C-1010. | VERIFIED | H | `lctbl_names.py`; weight table from `0x03485400`/`0x03485500`; EXP-2026-11-06-M3.9-name-collation |

## M2.13b — SDHC.dll CMD42 flags and boot password source (C-1950–C-1959)

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-1950 | `SDHC.dll` sec 0 (`.text`, rva `0x1000`, flags `0x60000020`) is not compressed in images B and C; only its two data sections carry the `0x2000` flag. | VERIFIED | H | `cerom.py sections SDHC.dll` (B, C); EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42 |
| C-1951 | Disk IOCTLs map to CMD42 flags: `0x80032014` SET_PWD `0x01`, `0x80032018` LOCK `0x04`, `0x8003201C` UNLOCK `0x00`, `0x80032020` CLR_PWD `0x02`, `0x80032024` forced ERASE `0x08` (`CDSKMain` vtable `0x039b1c30` slots 107–111, B; same in C). | VERIFIED | H | objdump of B/C `.text`; EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42 |
| C-1952 | The IOCTL wrappers pass the caller's buffer and `(u8)nInBufSize` unchanged to `CSDHCMain::SDHC_Lock_Unlock_Card` (vtable slot 108, `0x039b7df4`), so the card password is exactly the caller's bytes (16 for LeafSDTools, C-1945). | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42; C-792 |
| C-1953 | The driver builds the spec CMD42 frame: command byte `0x6a`, data `[flag & 0x0F, len, password]`, block length `len + 2` (1 for ERASE) then restored; it accepts flags `0,1,2,4,5,8`, `len <= 16`, and checks command class 7. | VERIFIED | H | `0x039baa0c`, `0x039bc050` (B); EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42 |
| C-1954 | At boot `CSDHCMain` slot 109 (`0x039b6ac4`, called from `CDSKMain::Init`) fetches 16 bytes through import slot `0x01f38050` with code `0x01013008` and sends UNLOCK (`0x00`, len 16) with them, in one branch after a LOCK (`0x04`). | OBSERVED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42 |
| C-1955 | `0x01013008` is `CTL_CODE(FILE_DEVICE_HAL, 0xC02)`; in `nk.exe` (B) its table record `{0x01013008, 1, 0x8804a568}` at `0x88009220` leads to a 16-byte copy from RAM `0x89e439b8 + 0x100`. That this RAM is the PROD block shadow is a hypothesis matching C-1945. | OBSERVED / HYPOTHESIS | M | EXP-2026-11-06-M2.13b-sdhc-cmd42 |
| C-1956 | The 4 unit bytes are stored per unit (PROD `+0x100`), not derived at runtime on this path; a card given the same 16 bytes by SET_PWD or SET_PWD+LOCK (Pico `setlock`) is in the state the boot unlock expects, so an independently locked card is feasible. | HYPOTHESIS | M | C-1951–C-1955, C-1945; bench test M6.9/M6.5 |
| C-1957 | LeafSDTools "SD LOCK" (`0x80032014`) is SET_PWD only, so the card locks at the next power-up; its "SD UNLOCK" (`0x80032020`) is CLR_PWD, which removes the password for good. | VERIFIED | H | C-1951; EXP-2026-11-06-M6.11-2-leafsdtools |

The on-disk `HDD_CHK.ATT` layout and the source/population of `ctx+120` are UNKNOWN from static analysis. | OBSERVED / UNKNOWN | M | EXP-2026-11-06-M2.12-hdd-chk-att |

Only `MapUpdate.exe` in B references `HDD_CHK.ATT` by string in IMG256. | VERIFIED | H | `sh4xref --to 0x0001c210`; string map; EXP-2026-11-06-M2.12-hdd-chk-att |

## How to verify

Every active claim names an `EXP-...` record; this table gives the exact
repo command where one exists. `<img>` is a raw SD image (vendor data, kept
outside the repo), `<root>` an extracted card root, `<tile>` a `BxxRyyR.DAT`.

| Active claims | Command | Expected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C-543–C-547, C-553, C-555, C-557, C-560–C-563 | `python3 tools/selftest.py` (or `--only <tool>`) | every `tool:*` suite `PASS`; `selftest: OK` |
| C-558–C-559 | `python3 tools/l3_harness/selftest.py`; `python3 tools/l3_harness/runner.py run …` | `selftest: OK (19 checks)`; `MISSING_GHIDRA` exit 3 until Ghidra is installed |
| C-546–C-547 | `python3 tools/section4.py verify SECTION4.bin` | parse→preserve byte-identical; `build` exit 3 with the unknown-field list |
| C-070–C-084, C-120–C-126, C-509–C-514 | no local command: external teardown/upstream sources only | see `sources.md`/`feedback-loop.md`; not reproduced here |
| C-110–C-116, C-132–C-142, C-150–C-156, C-210–C-216 | helpers `coord.py`/`poipoint.py`/`decode.py` are **not in `tools/`**; use `l2check.py` as the regression gate | original method in the named EXP |

## How to use this ledger

- When you verify or refute a claim, update its row and add the command/output
  to the evidence column.
- When you add a new claim, give it the next free ID in its section.
- Keep `docs/format/*.md` consistent with this file; the ledger wins if they
  disagree.
- Do not promote `OBSERVED`/`HYPOTHESIS` to `VERIFIED` without a reproducible
  check that another agent can run.

| C-1740 | On `IMG256` build A (`--image-start 0x200200`, sha256 `92aa525d…007f9`), `cerom.py comphdr --all` parses all 245 compressed sections as HOLDS with 0 REFUTED and 0 UNPARSED: 187 form 1 (22-byte, M1.2a) and 58 form 2 (multi-block, M1.2d). | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M1.2d-comphdr-form2; `python3 tools/cerom.py --image-start 0x200200 /tmp/opencode/img256.img comphdr --all --json`; `tools/realcheck.py` 8/8 PASS |
| C-1741 | Form 2 (all 58 former REFUTED sections) is a **multi-block compressed** framing, not stored and not a different block codec: a table of `n` little-endian `u24` values (`u24[0]` = uncompressed data length `<= vsize`, `u24[n-1] == psize`, `n >= 3`) plus a 16-byte trailer `[u32 0x10, u32 0x1000, u32 block_bytes, u32 0x1000]` (total header `3*n+16` bytes), then the payload at `3*n+16` starting with the CE LZXD block marker `0x8d80805b` (`5b 80 80 8d`). The payload contains exactly `n-1` such block markers (58/58). Supersedes C-1713. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M1.2d-comphdr-form2; IMG256 structural scan (58/58: last `u24==psize`, trailer `[0x10,0x1000,*,0x1000]`, marker present, `marker_count==n-1`, `u24[0]<=vsize`) |
| C-1742 | The 58 form-2 sections span header sizes 25–247 bytes (`n` 3–77, block counts 2–76). In 21/58 sections `u24[0] < vsize` (actual uncompressed data length, allocation is `vsize`); in 37/58 `u24[0] == vsize`. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M1.2d-comphdr-form2; `comphdr --all --json` on IMG256 |

| C-1750 | Root cause of C-1714 (`cerom.py thunks` "no import stub matched" on real image A): Navi.exe `o32.realaddr`/import slot VAs are module-local (`vbase=0x00010000`, `.text realaddr=0x00011000`, IAT slots `0x0055d000+`), not ROM VAs (`0x8cae1000+`). | C-1751 | `scan_section_stubs` now reads PC-relative literals via section `realaddr`/`dataptr` mapping (`read_u32_realaddr`) and finds stubs/call sites in modules whose VAs are module-local. `CERom.va2file` falls back to `realaddr2file` (o32 `realaddr` ranges, recursion-guarded), so `va2file 0x003d1bb0` on IMG256 resolves to `0x0104fdb0`. | VERIFIED (tool, synthetic + IMG256) | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M1.6c |
| C-1752 | The 4-insn IAT stub matcher is register-flexible (`mov.l @(pc),Rn; mov.l @Rn,Rn; jmp @Rn; nop` for any `Rn`) and `thunks_report` also detects inlined IAT call sites: a PC-rel load of a known import slot followed within a window by `mov.l @load_reg,Rm; jmp/jsr @Rm` (cross-register deref allowed, arg-setup interleaving tolerated). Non-IAT 2/3-insn direct patterns are suppressed to avoid false positives. | VERIFIED (tool) | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M1.6c; `cerom.py --selftest` |

The on-disk file is `LTNTBL.DAT`. `tools/drstr_ze0.build()` (pre-fix) produced `LTNTBL.DAT` by substituting the disk name after an exact-case order lookup missed the entry, yielding 9 byte diffs at `0x1ae0..0x1ae9` (C-1982). | VERIFIED (cause) | H | EXP-2026-11-06-M4.20-us-drstr-roundtrip |

## M3.11 second pass — POINT walker in sec0 (C-2540–C-2549)

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-2540 | `Navi.exe` (A) `fn 0x003f5194(r4=page, r5=n)` returns a pointer to record *n* of a page or 0: header = native u16 `w`; `len = 0x4e8` words if `(w & 0xfc00) == 0xfc00`, else `w & 0x3ff` words; stops on `len == 0` or after 20000 records; advances by `2*len`. It calls nothing. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-07-M3.11-point-reader-2; disasm `0x3f5194–0x3f51d8` |
| C-2541 | Executed from the ROM bytes in PySh4 (little-endian) on US `POINT010.DAT` page `0x840ba0` (130,178 B, swapped to native u16), `0x3f5194` returns the `parse_page` offset for n = 0,1,2,3,10,500,998,999 and 0 for n = 1000; on the unswapped page it returns `0xc0`/`0x4c0` for n = 1/2 (expected `0x80`/`0xf8`). | VERIFIED | H | `point_walk_l3.py walk` |
| C-2542 | The POINT `tag,len2` pair is one big-endian u16: type = top 6 bits (`0x60`→`0x18`, `0x68`→`0x1a`), length = low 10 bits in words, so a tag's two low bits are length bits 8–9 and tags `0xfc–0xff` are the escape. Writers must keep `len ≤ 0x3ff` words and never emit an `0xfc..` tag. | VERIFIED (walker semantics) / INFERENCE (writer rule) | H | C-2540; `point_walk_l3.py --selftest` |
| C-2543 | `fn 0x003f5cdc(page, n)` dispatches to `0x3f5b88` / `0x3f5194` / `0x3f39c0` by comparing `page` with buffer slots of the table at `0x00d53500`; `fn 0x003f39c0(p, n)` skips n records only if `p == *0x00d5e4e4` and `n < *0x00d5e4d0`; `fn 0x003f3314(&count, &base)` returns those two globals; `fn 0x003f8a0c(rec)` tests the `0xfc00` escape; `fn 0x003f5c40` yields `rec+4` for a normal record; `fn 0x003f5c90` copies the last 8 bytes of a record. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-07-M3.11-point-reader-2; disasm, `sh4xref --to` |

Name accessor **`fn 0x003d1150(ctx, rec, &ptr)`**: `len = rec[14]`, `ptr = rec+21`; if `rec[14] == 0`, `fn 0x003d09a0` splits text at `rec+191` (length `rec[19]`) on the separator byte at `*0x005628e8`. `fn 0x003d1188` copies the name (≤256 B). | OBSERVED | M | EXP-2026-11-07-M3.11-point-reader-2; disasm |

## M9.21 — PySh4 little-endian and bt/s, bf/s (C-2550–C-2559)

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-2550 | `PySh4(endian=...)` in `tools/l3_harness/engine.py`: `endian="big"` is the default and keeps the old behaviour; `"little"` makes `_load`, `_store` and `_fetch` little-endian; any other value raises `UsageError`. Supersedes C-2546 for the engine's current state. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M9.21-pysh4-le; `engine.py --selftest` ("endian defaults to big, selects little, rejects other values") |
| C-2551 | `bt/s` and `bf/s` are modelled in `PySh4` with the delay-slot rule of `bra`: the slot runs once in both outcomes; a taken branch continues at its target, an untaken one at pc+4. The condition is read at the branch (a slot that writes T does not change the outcome). One self-test vector per opcode, in both byte orders and both outcomes, plus the T-writing slot. The SH-4 manual was not consulted (no link in `docs/research/sources.md`); the rule is checked by C-2552. | VERIFIED (self-test) / HYPOTHESIS (manual rule) | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M9.21-pysh4-le; `engine.py --selftest` ("bf/s ...", "bt/s ...") |
| C-2552 | On real bytes, `fn 0x003f39c0(p, n)` (which runs a `bf/s` loop at `0x3f39f2` per skipped record) returns the `parse_page` offset for `n` = 0, 1, 2, 3, 10, 500, 998, 999 and 0 for `n` = 1000, on US `POINT010.DAT` page `0x840ba0` (130,178 B, 1000 records), with the two globals set by the tool. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M9.21-pysh4-le; `point_walk_l3.py skip` (result: PASS) |
| C-2553 | The two globals read by `fn 0x003f39c0` are `*0x00d5e4d0` (record count) and `*0x00d5e4e4` (current page base). Literal loads at `0x3f39c2` and `0x3f39ca` give their addresses; the guard tests the count first (`cmp/hs`, `0x3f39c6`) and then `p == base` (`cmp/eq`, `0x3f39ce`). With the count cell at 500 the function returns record 499 for `n` = 499 and 0 for `n` = 500. Their values in a booted unit are not read from the image (sec1 is compressed, C-2128); the tool sets them to 1000 and `0x20000000`. | VERIFIED (addresses, disassembly; count control) / UNKNOWN (runtime values) | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M9.21-pysh4-le; `point_walk_l3.py skip`; `sh4xref.py --calls 0x3f39c0` (no callee) |

| C-2555 | `tools/point_walk_l3.py` runs `PySh4(endian="little")` directly; the local `PySh4LE` subclass and `le_engine()` are removed. Unit 0 passes the `rd_check.check` body test on 65,273 of 65,274 level-1 records of B48R058R, 540/540 of the pinned level-1 records, 90/90 level-2 part-a records, 379/380 level-4 and 34/34 level-5. | VERIFIED (tool, real data) | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M4.23-restriction-encoding (Observations); `tools/rd_restrictions.py census` |

The call target is the pointer stored at `0x0055d924` in compressed sec1, which is not readable from decodable bytes; whether that pointer leads to plain or compressed code is UNKNOWN. The `0x0040b358` call at `0x00412614` (literal, then `jsr @r3` with no dereference) is direct. Supersedes C-2201 (direct-call clause) and C-2202 (shared-callee reading). | VERIFIED (counts) | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M1.12-reader-precheck; `tools/reader_precheck.py --table` |

## M5.21 — Slot budget for generated POI pages (C-2580–C-2589)

| ID | Claim | Status | Confidence | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C-2580 | A POI name page may grow only into the zero bytes after its own zlib stream, up to the next 0x100 boundary; if a non-zero byte comes first, the slot is the stream length (`slot_budget.slot_at`). A zlib stream never starts with 0x00, so a zero run cannot hide a page start. On the US POINT001–011 factory pages, 8,113 of 8,229 edited pages (98.6 %) abut the next page with zero slack. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget; `slot_budget.py --selftest` (abut and padding checks); `build` budget rows (`slot == stream`) |
| C-2581 | Stock zlib 1.3.2 (levels 1–9 × memLevel 8/9) re-encodes none of the 31,371 US POINT001–011 zlib pages larger than its factory stream (1,715 equal length, 29,656 smaller). zlib-ng 1.3.1 re-encodes 1,867 pages larger, by up to 734 B (3.64 % on POINT010). | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Observations); `slot_budget.py calibrate` (`calibrate.json`, `calibrate-zlibng.json`) |
| C-2582 | Headroom is 4 % of the factory stream (`HEADROOM_RATIO`). It is a portability margin: it covers the largest measured non-stock excess (3.64 %, C-2581), so a build from a non-stock zlib gives the same fit decisions. It does not make abutting pages fit. | VERIFIED | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Interpretation); `slot_budget.py build --headroom 0.04` |
| C-2583 | Build at 4 % headroom, names replaced with hash-derived same-length text: 8,229 pages carry a `NAME@` edit (5,891,589 found); 19 pages fit and carry 13,749 names (≥ 1,000); 8,210 pages are refused and left unchanged, listed in `*.refused.json` (overflow sum 35,172,654 B; median 4,282 B). | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Observations); `slot_budget.py build --headroom 0.04 --image …` |
| C-2584 | At 0 % headroom the same edit set fits 286 pages and applies 152,085 names (2.6 %); 7,943 pages are refused. The packing, not the headroom, is the main limit. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget; `slot_budget.py build --headroom 0` |
| C-2585 | Decoded-domain check of the 4 % build against the factory: every changed byte lies inside a fitted page's slot (0 outside), and all 13,749 changed names read back as the generator's output followed by `@` (0 failures). Applied name lengths are 4–38 (4: 77.9 %, 5: 19.3 %). | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Observations); `slot_budget.py verify --factory … --built …` (`verify: PASS`) |
| C-2586 | The 4 % card (`card.img`, SHA-256 `8c9111c5…caba`) passes `cardcheck.py --hash` (RESULT PASS). Its diff against the factory US image (SHA-256 `53342430…c5383`) lies in the data area for the first 100,000 ranges (25,437,115 B); the count is capped, so the full diff is not counted. | VERIFIED | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget; `cardcheck.py --image … --hash`; `mkcard.diff_ranges(…, limit=100000)` |
| C-2587 | Control: a no-op rewrite of POINT009 (no edits) through `slot_budget.write_pair` returns the factory `.cmp` and `.ind` byte-for-byte; the selftest's incompressible 3,022 B page into a 52 B slot is refused with its bytes unchanged. | VERIFIED | H | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Method 6); `slot_budget.py --selftest` (14/14) |
| C-2588 | The encoder is not the limit: the budget tries the factory encoder (stock zlib L6/memLevel 8, M1.13) and the smallest of the 18 stock settings (levels 1–9 × memLevel 8/9); none of the 8,210 refused pages fits under any of them. Other zlib parameters (strategy, window bits) were not tried. | VERIFIED | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M5.21-slot-budget (Interpretation); `slot_budget.py build` (refused rows: `new_stream > limit` for the smallest stock stream); M1.13 |

Callers pass `ctx = caller->+8` (`fn 0x1ccfc` @`0x1cdd4` r5=1 SystemSD, `0x1ce46` r5=0 UserSD; `fn 0x1cee8` @`0x1d002` r5=0), so the field is the caller subobject `+120`. HDD_INFO reader refs exist in other fns (`0x1b0fa/0x1bad0/0x1c3ec/0x1d268`, C-2616) but the **population/dataflow of `ctx+120` (20-hex CRC16==0 value origin/generation rule) is not statically closed**. | VERIFIED (read site/callers) / UNKNOWN (population) | M | EXP-2026-11-08-M2.17-hddchk-crypto-ctx; `sh4xref --to 0x1c210`; `sh4dis --va 0x1c210 --count 120`, `--va 0x1ccfc` |
